Retries are permitted only before the first complete payload for the exact same source object/request, preserving source family, applicable period, requested URL, request parameters, and provider/domain exactly -- no alternate URL/provider/mirror/month/suffix/parameter change. Retryable transport classes are exactly `NETWORK_TIMEOUT`, `CONNECTION_RESET`, `TEMPORARY_DNS_FAILURE`, `HTTP_408`, `HTTP_425`, `HTTP_429`, `HTTP_500`, `HTTP_502`, `HTTP_503`, `HTTP_504`; no other condition is retryable, and nonretryable conditions (off-domain/untrusted redirect, response-host mismatch, parser/schema failure, semantic/data-quality failure, missing/ambiguous evidence, governance/implementation failure) stop immediately with no attempts consumed.

- New GPT prefreeze methodology decision recorded (docs-only; not yet implemented in code): `V9_006_STAGE_A_SEMANTIC_VALIDATION_METHODOLOGY.md` binds original V9_006 HIGH_2's (full semantic reconstruction/validation) exact methodology for `SECURITY_TYPE`, `CANONICAL_IDENTITY`, `LISTING_TRANSITIONS`/`DELISTING_TRANSITIONS`/`MARKET_TRANSITIONS`, `EFFECTIVE_DATE`, and `RECONSTRUCTION`. `canonical_security_identity = canonical_code`: the exact 4-character canonical code, UTF-8/ASCII, no prefix/suffix/company-name/market/date; only unambiguous spreadsheet-artifact stripping (whitespace, integral `.0`) and ASCII uppercasing are permitted; JPX's 2024+ alphanumeric codes are explicitly in scope (not digits-only), and a 5-character code using a reserved security-type character is never the canonical ordinary-common identity. `AMBIGUOUS_REUSED_SECURITY_CODE` fails Stage A when the same code represents more than one disjoint genuine listing episode, with no company-name/future-data/outcome disambiguation; a mere continuously-listed name change is not a new identity. Point-in-time `listed_state`/market-state/`security_type_state` reconstruction uses exact half-open effective-date semantics (`listed_state=false` strictly before a listing date, `true` on/after; the mirror for delisting); missing/ambiguous dates fail `effective_date_pass`, and conflicting same-code/same-date transitions without a mechanically unique official ordering fail (no manual ordering). `security_type_state` is exactly `ELIGIBLE_DOMESTIC_ORDINARY_COMMON` / `EXPLICITLY_INELIGIBLE` / `UNKNOWN`; `ELIGIBLE` requires official proof of both domestic/TSE listing category and ordinary-common-stock code form; ETF/ETP/REIT/foreign/preferred/class/special/new-share products are `EXPLICITLY_INELIGIBLE`; any `UNKNOWN` needed for a reconstructed identity/date fails `security_type_pass`; eligibility must never be inferred from price availability, TOPIX membership, survival, name text, today's state, or outcomes. `listing_transition_pass`/`delisting_transition_pass` must be computed from parsed official transition events (not merely F2 family coverage, and F2/F3 conflicts fail); `market_transition_pass` must independently prove every encountered transition's exact effective date and must not merely proxy `listing_transition_pass`. `canonical_identity_pass` requires valid-grammar codes, no same-date duplicates, and no ambiguous/reused-code condition for every reconstructed candidate identity. `deterministic_reconstruction_pass` adds a reverse/forward consistency check (replay backward through the chronology then forward using the same canonical events; the recovered terminal state must byte-match the canonical terminal state, no reconciliation on mismatch) in addition to the existing byte-identical-repeated-run requirement.

- `src/v9_005_stage_a_jpx_probe.py` is rewired to consume this engine: `reconstruct_security_state`/`reconstruction_is_deterministic` now take `terminal_identities`/`events` and delegate to the semantics engine, replacing the placeholder that always reported `reconstructed_identity_count=0`. `compute_stage_a_evidence` now takes a `semantic_result` mapping and feeds its seven semantic booleans directly into the `FREE_JPX_METADATA_PROBE_PASS` conjunction; the old `_family_fully_covered`-based proxies (`listing_transition_pass==F2 coverage`, `delisting_transition_pass==F3 coverage`, `market_transition_pass==listing_transition_pass`, `canonical_identity_pass==terminal_snapshot_locked AND security_type_pass`, `effective_date_pass` as a coverage conjunction) are all removed; `terminal_snapshot_pass`/`trading_calendar_pass` are unchanged (not semantic-evidence items). `run_stage_a()` calls `compute_semantic_validation_result(terminal_identities={}, events=())` explicitly -- production has no acquired F2-F7 semantic evidence and this call site remains unreachable today (blocked by `verify_acquisition_implementation_ready()` first) -- so the engine's own fail-closed empty-input default governs, never a hardcoded or caller-supplied arbitrary PASS boolean.

- Finding 1: `compute_semantic_validation_result` never validated a `terminal_identities` entry's shape or field types, and normalized-code collisions (e.g. `"1301"` vs. `" 1301 "`, or `"130a"` vs. `"130A"`) were resolved by silent dict-assignment overwrite (`identities[code] = state`) rather than being rejected -- a caller could smuggle a non-`TerminalIdentityState` value, a non-bool `listed_state` (including a truthy int like `1`), an empty/non-string `market_state`, or an out-of-enum `security_type_state` straight into semantic reconstruction, and a genuine code collision would silently resolve to whichever entry was inserted last.
- Remediation (`src/v9_005_stage_a_semantics.py` only -- `src/v9_005_stage_a_jpx_probe.py` not touched): a new `_validate_terminal_identity_state` helper independently checks `listed_state` (must be an actual `bool` -- `isinstance(1, bool)` is `False`, so a truthy int is correctly rejected), `market_state` (non-empty `str`, no market enumeration invented), and `security_type_state` (exactly one of `VALID_SECURITY_TYPE_STATES`); a non-`TerminalIdentityState` value fails all three. Each field's invalidity fails closed exactly the specified evidence items: invalid `listed_state` -> `listing_transition_pass`/`delisting_transition_pass`/`canonical_identity_pass`/`deterministic_reconstruction_pass`; invalid `market_state` -> `market_transition_pass`/`canonical_identity_pass`/`deterministic_reconstruction_pass`; invalid `security_type_state` -> `security_type_pass`/`canonical_identity_pass`/`deterministic_reconstruction_pass`. Canonical-code collision detection now groups raw `terminal_identities` keys by normalized `canonical_code` first; any code with more than one distinct raw key is `DUPLICATE_CANONICAL_IDENTITY` -- neither entry is used, and `canonical_identity_pass`/`deterministic_reconstruction_pass` both fail, even when the colliding states are byte-identical. The exact canonical-code grammar and all other previously reviewed methodology (events, conflict/corroboration dedup, reused-code detection, reverse/forward reconstruction, UNKNOWN-while-listed) are unchanged; orphan-event-identity rejection and the two-run determinism gate were explicitly not touched.
- `tests/test_v9_005_stage_a_semantics.py` grows from 45 to 57 offline tests, adding coverage for an out-of-enum `security_type_state`, an empty/non-string `market_state`, `listed_state` as int `1`/`0`, a non-`TerminalIdentityState` mapping value, whitespace- and case-normalized code collisions, proof that a collision is never silently overwritten (neither raw key's state survives into `canonical_state`/`reconstructed_identity_count`), proof that byte-identical colliding states still fail, and a regression check that a normal single-key valid terminal identity behaves unchanged. All 45 pre-existing tests continue to pass; zero real network (structurally verified -- the module has no network-capable imports).

Since every field now resolves to a closed enum or bounded integer, no allowed key -- top-level or nested -- can carry arbitrary free-form text. The reviewed allowed top-level key set is unchanged (no key added or removed); every rejection still raises through the same `_blocked(..., raw_bytes_read_for_integrity=True, child_content_inspected=True)` path, preserving the MEDIUM-1 phase-provenance contract. `_default_structural_inspector`'s output already satisfies the strict schema unchanged, so default production behavior is preserved exactly. - Finding: the prior MEDIUM-3 remediation's five closed-set membership checks (`status` against `OUTCOMES`; `container_format`; `open_parse_status`; a dimension's `visibility`; a dimension's `object_type`) each used a bare `value not in some_frozenset` test. `in` on a `frozenset` hashes its operand first, so an unhashable malformed value (a `list` or `dict`, e.g. `status=[]`, `container_format={}`) raises a raw `TypeError` from inside the membership test itself, before the intended `ProbeBlocked` path is reached -- reproduced directly: `_safe_structural_evidence({"status": []})` raised `TypeError: unhashable type: 'list'`. Because this can occur after the Phase-C structural-inspection boundary has already been crossed, an escaping `TypeError` would be caught only by the CLI's generic non-`ProbeBlocked` fallback, incorrectly reporting `raw_bytes_read_for_integrity="unknown"`/`child_content_inspected=false` for a failure that in fact occurred after the CHILD bytes were read and structural inspection had begun.
- Remediation in `src/v9_006_f6_offline_child_structural_probe.py` adds `_is_allowed_enum_str(value, allowed) -> bool` (`isinstance(value, str) and value in allowed`), making every closed-set membership check total for arbitrary Python objects, applied to all five enum checks (`status`, `container_format`, `open_parse_status`, `visibility`, `object_type`). No enum member, allowed key, or evidence category was added, removed, or broadened -- the exact schema from the prior MEDIUM-3 remediation is preserved unchanged. As defense in depth, `run_offline_child_structural_probe`'s Phase-C boundary now also wraps the `_safe_structural_evidence(...)` call inside the same `try`/`except ProbeBlocked`/`except Exception` block already used for the `structural_inspector(...)` call (previously called unprotected, after that block); any unexpected exception during safe-evidence validation -- not only the fixed unhashable-value case -- is now translated to `ProbeBlocked(IMPLEMENTATION_FAILURE, raw_bytes_read_for_integrity=True, child_content_inspected=True)` rather than escaping. An already-correct `ProbeBlocked` from `_safe_structural_evidence` is still re-raised the same way as before, preserving its outcome and true/true fields.

- Frozen methodology: (2) before any `DATE` cell value is read, the reviewed structural inspector's evidence must be recomputed, canonicalized (`json.dumps(evidence, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":")).encode("utf-8")`), SHA-256-hashed, and compared to the frozen `4332d0b27a1e35256abef4c0e240b2c576c20122a264374ea0c5da3729beacce` -- any mismatch is `IMPLEMENTATION_FAILURE` with no `DATE` value read; (3) the only date-bearing columns are the preregistered 1-based ordinals `4` and `6` on sheet `1`, never selected or searched for; (4) the exact same reviewed `xlrd.open_workbook(file_contents=..., formatting_info=True, on_demand=False, ragged_rows=False)` call reopens the verified bytes; (5) for each column independently, every row is inspected via `cell_type` first, `cell_value` is read only when `cell_type == xlrd.XL_CELL_DATE`, and `xlrd.xldate_as_tuple(serial, book.datemode)[0]` extracts only the integer year -- never the serial, month, day, or time; the encountered `DATE` count per column must equal that column's own hash-verified structural-evidence count, or `IMPLEMENTATION_FAILURE`; (6) a deterministic per-column year histogram (`{year, count}`, ascending) is built from parsed `DATE` cells only; (7) the two columns' complete histograms must be exactly identical or the result is `F6_YEAR_COVERAGE_AMBIGUOUS` with no covered-year set accepted and no union/intersection/majority-vote/nearest-match/manual resolution permitted, returned to GPT for a methodology decision; (8) when identical, `covered_years`/`covered_required_years`/`missing_required_years`/`all_required_years_covered` are derived against the fixed `2017..2025` required-years set from `V9_006_STAGE_A_F6_GLOBAL_COVERAGE_METHODOLOGY.md`, a required year proven only by appearing in both columns' histograms.
